Theorem 219.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The informatics variety V defined by convergence analysis constraints admits a stratification into 328 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 249 and intersection form of rank 3.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to informatics theory, we decompose the problem into 328 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 249. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(3/n). QED.

Theorem 219.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Every structural decomposition deformation of the informatics object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 152. The moduli space has dimension at most 18 with 1 connected components.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 152 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the informatics structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 18. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
